# Buddha: consolidated living plan

Revision 356 - November 13, 2026. **This is the single authoritative roadmap for scope, order, status, dependencies and the next slice.** It combines the original research/design plan, completed implementation, live-pilot findings and the interrupted attempt. Preserve original objectives and frozen evidence. Update this plan as evidence changes.

[How Buddha works](HOW-IT-WORKS.md) explains the workflow, scoring and judge-audit findings in plain English. [DESIGN.md](DESIGN.md) supplies the technical and scientific contract. [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md) and [NOVELTYBENCH.md](NOVELTYBENCH.md) supply source-backed context. Run manifests, receipts, results and dated reviews supply evidence. Those documents retain their roles; their old recommendations and slice numbers do not override this plan's current sequence.

**Where we are (November 13).** V5 stopped in development on an Anthropic HTTP 503 during utility scoring. Keep 637 settled operations, $5.600044 spent, the $11.44 hold and the stopped task's sealed answers and partition. Four common screens and 11 of 16 improvement opportunities are complete. The durable replacement path now records a single reviewed attempt and keeps the first hold. Short accounting, utility replay, final-funding and relocated-source checks pass. Held Request Recovery blocks unexpected callbacks and keeps the frozen Sonnet refusal fallback subject to the original limits. The November 13 provider-blocked rehearsal completed synthetic development on unchanged source. A development directory inventory check stopped final schedule verification before the first final worker. A short offline check reproduced the stop from a directory clock change and verified the fix under the final lease. Read-only checks before and after matched all 638 V5 ledger records and the original ledger file hashes. Actual replay of the 637 settled calls remains unverified. V5 stays paused pending verification through the first final worker and separate live approval. [Resume V5](D8-LIVE-COMPARISON.md#resume-v5).

The remaining workload check found two concrete preparation gaps: the evolved actor still had a separate 1 MiB per-note guard, and a fixed 32 MiB cumulative worker-output allowance could be exhausted by several individually valid callbacks. The prospective Ccompat2 changes only that remaining per-note guard to 32 MiB; historical C and the earlier Ccompat remain unchanged. The existing preparation helper now derives cumulative stdout from the configured callback count plus three protocol frames, retaining any larger existing allowance. Twelve callbacks therefore allow at least 480 MiB cumulatively. Per-frame 32 MiB, per-request 16 MiB, memory 256 MiB and existing 900/1,800-second worker limits are unchanged. This raises a stream byte counter, not an in-memory allocation.

Six short checks pass in 1.9885 seconds, including the existing executor with a supplied Docker fixture at the 16 MiB callback-body limit and four requests exceeding 32 MiB of cumulative output. These checks use no real containers or model calls. The selected P3/Ccompat2 pair then passes four short confined sessions in 26.370 seconds within 120 seconds, including a 1 MiB parent source and complete thirty-current/thirty-parent answer feedback. The largest request body is 12,694,920 bytes, below 16 MiB; every owned container exits successfully and is removed. Provider replies and usage are supplied, so this is transport evidence, not live model-context evidence. The four existing metadata builders now complete in 22.729 seconds within 120 seconds, producing fresh unfunded v3 primary/plain/review/combined packages. Independent output review passes for all 133 indexed artifacts and all source bindings. Hosted short CI passes all 517 methods per OS and all 14 quality checks at `d0f5cb6`. Fresh runtime `c28851f9a9d85b02e86ed14589de4877353561c7` pins all 348 Python files. All 84 core production files remain unchanged from `3214557`; four helper/test-support Python files differ from `5ab`, five from `3214557` including the earlier test-only correction. [Capacity delivery](conformance/d8-workload-capacity-delivery.json).

**Model context uses the route already allowed by DESIGN section 4:** use the now-adopted capacity policy v2 and check authoritative usage from each actual original proposal call before admitting its child. No tokenizer, bytes-as-tokens estimate or new qualification gate is introduced. A 32 MiB feedback ceiling is not a promise that every document of that size fits the complete request or model. Full outputs, exact request guards, complete replacement-source checks, accounting and necessary stops remain intact; future descendants are not certified in advance.

Windows masked entry and the historical handoff retain their recorded checks. The stopped recovery retains ten generation replies and its native-startup partial. The corrected producer/passive reader pass the actual deep-path host/evaluator check with paid access blocked; source8fa7dc9 passes both short CI jobs. The new handoff binds that source and only the remaining approved allowance. Both earlier commands are retired; keep all eight old grants read-only. No old runtime, answer or ledger row was rewritten.

## Delivery reset: the current work in plain English

**The deliverable is still one usable loop: generate ten answers, score them with the official NoveltyBench evaluator, give complete development feedback to the inherited improver, execute its changed Python, and compare the result.** The larger controlled comparison has not completed. Official evaluation and actual source-changing children have been demonstrated; better scores and a better improver have not. The completed source experiment scored worse. Infrastructure test passes do not close that gap.

**Current delivery rule — continue Buddha; exact DGM adoption out of scope.** The user's latest decision follows consideration of direct DGM reuse and an implementation hold: proceed with our existing code, keeping the science rigorous. Buddha remains DGM-inspired, not a DGM replication. Apply Ponytail/Gall's Law to the smallest existing generate → official score → full feedback → source mutate → compare path. Reuse source_screen generation/official evaluation, complete feedback and candidate-improvement components; run_source_search still reconstructs history and is not a solved shortcut. Address the shared forward-execution versus historical-reader boundary, not a cosmetic file move, an incremental-replay engine, a parallel controller or an additional orchestration framework. Keep accounting, isolation and official scoring intact. Exact DGM fork/vendor/adapter work is out of scope; keep DGM as a workflow reference; no paid calls, new long runs or retries are authorized.

**Scientific contract for the next path.** Preserve the pinned official NoveltyBench1.1 code and judge settings. Keep generator choice configurable, with LOW effort by default for reasoning models; verify actual outgoing requests before live calls. Match generator/model, sampling and resource allowances against the planned controls; retain prospective development/final separation, complete feedback, predeclared analysis and uncertainty, and all failures/negative outcomes. An infrastructure or small engineering pass is not evidence of score improvement or a better improver. The original three-development/26-final-task comparison and both plain controls remain open; a smaller engineering check cannot replace them. Future paid work still needs a fresh checked handoff, funding and exact-source qualification.

## Objective and separate completion claims

Build an agent that improves its procedures for producing ten useful, meaningfully different answers on NoveltyBench or a separately declared related creativity benchmark. Retain the original endpoint: actual task-agent and improvement-procedure source evolution, not only stronger prompts.

| Claim | Required evidence | Current state |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Working experiment infrastructure | Real generation, evaluation, accounting, provenance, interruption and recovery checks within the claimed scope | Fresh pilot and linked continuation completed and verified; bounded recovery/verification/observation gates complete; scientific claims below remain open |
| Useful diversity improves | Frozen agent on unseen prompts, matched baselines/resources, both metrics, task compliance, independent judgment audit and uncertainty | Not established |
| Source self-modification works | Parent-authored child changes real agent code and runs through a validated isolated executor | Complete at original engineering scope: the [current-source experiment](SOURCE-CURRENT-LIVE-RESULTS.md) demonstrates actual changed generation and further-child production with full measured feedback, literal model-source/private-copy joins and separate executed-use reviews. Semantic compliance and improved task performance remain separate; current C scores lower than P. The [historical three-child chain](SOURCE-FIRST-LIVE-RESULTS.md) remains unchanged. |
| The improver improves | Initial versus evolved improvers produce descendants from the same starting conditions under equal budgets, assessed on separate sealed tasks | Not established. The current changed inherited C improver actually authored W1 from complete C feedback; that closes SRC-03 mechanism use, not effectiveness. Earlier versioned finite development, paired analysis, aggregate funding and protected sealed assessment infrastructure passed hosted verification at their recorded published cuts. The [bounded v3 sealed-grid rehearsal](conformance/imp01-v3-sealed-rehearsal-delivery.json) passes with supplied workers/replies; the earlier prepared source passed all eight exact-source hosted jobs at its recorded cut; subsequent source/recovery retains separate gates. The original bounded IMP-01 scope was adopted and funded, then stopped as a partial. Fresh-comparison preparation retains its scientific questions but needs separate funding and a new checked handoff; actual full-grid outcomes, empirical fairness and improver effectiveness remain unmeasured. |

[The backlog context](BACKLOG.md#dgm-01-complete-inherited-source-self-improvement) records why completing inherited Python source evolution remains original scope under SRC-02/03 and IMP-01; the first one-file execution mechanism does not close those broader gates. It supplies context, not a second roadmap.

Task-score improvement alone cannot close the last claim. A functioning loop, source mutation or rising best archive score cannot close the useful-diversity claim. Negative or inconclusive experiments remain valid records; they do not become positive claims to satisfy a roadmap milestone.

The first case alone takes 89m52s, 38.33% of the job's test time. Its public fixture copies an old host and can launch up to48 separate one-action recovery children before continuing development, final assessment, both plain modes and keyless replay. Each old-action child reconstructs/checks/finishes growing history before advancing; the bound48 is not a measured child count. Ordinary uninterrupted controller execution stays in one process, although it still reconstructs state before actions. This test's total is not an estimate of ordinary steady-state throughput. The second case executes six failure/admission opportunities and then inspects and replays the completed fenced study.

The first method's actual log records 74 successful subtests and one final success. The largest gap between recorded completions is 1,966.108seconds (32m46s). No structured start record was printed, so its initial monotonic gap is unknown; a separate unittest-start label is observed. This supports checked progress inside the long method and explains why total method duration alone cannot trigger the 60-minute inactivity rule. Source review confirms assertion-bearing subtests; heartbeat text does not advance that clock.

Fresh-per-old-step execution is a prefix-building mechanism rather than a process-count assertion, but it exercises cold imports, reconstruction and context reset. Combining those children would change exercised recovery boundaries, per-action180-second limits and progress delivery even if current value assertions still passed. Any future fixture optimization needs a separately checked coverage argument. No such change or profile is selected here. ## Work order and dependencies

Statuses are **next**, **planned**, **in progress**, **complete**, or **conditional**. In progress means implementation has started but its full gate remains open. “Next” identifies exactly one slice. Complete means the stated deliverable and gate have evidence, not that every broader research claim is true. Stable IDs remain unchanged when order changes.

DGM adoption is out of scope: reference only. Preserve the original3development/26final WildChat comparison, both plain controls, official NoveltyBench1.1/settings, complete comparative feedback, matched resources, capacity/mutation witnesses, prospective dev/final separation and predeclared analysis/uncertainty/failures. No paid calls/new long runs, scientific improvement or large-history pass is claimed. Original acceptance retained: Freeze initial and evolved improvers. Give them the same starting bundles and development packets, equal descendant budgets and selection rules across several starting conditions/repetitions. Assess selected descendants on a separate sealed task set. Report valid-child rate, target repair, net task gain, regressions, descendant productivity and improvement per budget; charge failed attempts. | SRC-03, EVAL-01/02 infrastructure, independent funding/analysis plan |

A better task agent is not evidence of a better improver. If causal claims concern reflective feedback, declare the appropriate no-feedback or shuffled-feedback comparison. Keep targeted repair, transfer and net held-out gain separate.

## Benchmark, research and delivery requirements that apply throughout

- The current official code/protocol governs new benchmark experiments; the paper is historical context. Existing runs stay on their frozen v1.1 reference. Select/install/pin a new release through [the modular integration contract](BENCHMARK-INTEGRATION.md); compatible updates use configuration/checks and the unchanged-agent baseline before optimization. Do not spread version branches into agent/search/accounting or rewrite old evidence. Reverify provider bounds and availability before live execution.
- Continue targeted research from credible primary scientific/academic/engineering sources when a pending decision needs it. Preserve source revisions, licensing and the distinction between published claims, inspected code and project hypotheses. Do not turn the roadmap into an indefinite literature survey.
- HyperAgents remains conceptual inspiration only; no implementation or implementation-specific prompt copying. Dependencies, repository code, papers and datasets have separate permissions. Resolve redistribution questions before distribution; permissive dependency defaults remain in force.
- NoveltyBench and a separately declared sealed transfer benchmark can support different claims. Never describe within-set difference as proof of historical originality or indefinite open-endedness.
- A contemporary Novamine/leaderboard comparison is **conditional later work**: refresh official reference/submission conditions and comparable evidence before making a claim. Full external comparability, reproducible artifacts and honest total cost are required. No leaderboard submission or publication occurs merely because it appears in this plan, and beating a leader is an empirical target rather than an infrastructure acceptance test.
- Keep the local design small. No queue service, Redis, vector database, multi-tenant backend, training framework or persistent multi-agent platform is required by the present evidence. Optional internal critics, adaptive selection, curricula and extra sampling mechanisms remain hypotheses drawn from the research register, not automatically scheduled features.
- Each implementation item starts with spec-defined failing checks, a concrete shape and the minimum correct implementation. The user now authorizes continuing implementation across slices, pausing only for a required live run or user decision. Relay a summary and create a conventional commit/PR for each small slice; then continue authorized work. Keep dependent PRs stacked without silently merging unreviewed work. Frozen experiments and paid scope remain protected.

## Keeping this plan current

For each new finding, record its evidence in the appropriate dated report, give it a stable finding ID, and link it to the affected work item and completion gate here. State whether it is an observation, reproduced defect, untested risk or proposed intervention. Reorder or split tasks when justified; retain stable IDs and a reason in the revision log. Do not treat every concern as a mandate to add a feature.

At each completed slice, update its status, evidence and remaining limits, set exactly one next slice, and summarize the change to the user. Claim states change only with their own evidence. Frozen manifests/receipts/results remain historical records; corrections use an explicit new version or linked assessment. Do not duplicate sequencing in incident reports, review handoffs or a second status file.

Git baseline `eacd68c` preserves the pre-recovery implementation in [wavemanco/buddha](https://github.com/wavemanco/buddha). Existing slices 1–3 were captured together because no earlier Git history existed; future slices have separate branches/PRs. CI is configured for Windows/Linux with pinned checking tools. Slice reports distinguish local checks from observed hosted-CI results.

Use the Current checkpoint and stable work items above for current scheduling. The [current configuration](UNIFIED-LIVE-CONFIGURATION.md) describes the prepared inputs; older draft checkpoints and dated reports remain historical evidence rather than competing instructions.
